## Introduction

Lar<sub>g</sub>e lan<sub>g</sub>ua<sub>g</sub>e models (LLMs) are increasin<sub>g</sub>l<sub>y</sub> used to <sub>s</sub>i<sub>mu</sub>l<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e po</sub>liti<sub>ca</sub>l <sub>an</sub>d i<sub>ns</sub>tit<sub>u</sub>ti<sub>ona</sub>l d<sub>ec</sub>i<sub>s</sub>i<sub>on-ma</sub>ki<sub>ng, coop-</sub> eration and resource mana<sub>g</sub>ement (Piatti et al. 2024), escalation (Hua et al. 2023; Rivera et al. 2024), collaborative law-makin<sub>g</sub> (Hota and Jokinen 2025), roll-call <sub>p</sub>rediction (Li, Gon<sub>g</sub>, and Jian<sub>g</sub> 2025; Grossmann et al. 2023). Unlike <sub>ru</sub>l<sub>e-</sub>b<sub>ase</sub>d <sub>mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l<sub>s,</sub> LLM <sub>agen</sub>t<sub>s</sub> <sub>reason</sub> i<sub>n</sub> <sub>na</sub>t<sub>ura</sub>l l<sub>anguage</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>s</sub>i<sub>mu</sub>l<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub> <sub>open-en</sub>d<sub>e</sub>d b<sub>e</sub>h<sub>av</sub>i<sub>our,</sub> b<sub>u</sub>t th<sub>e</sub>i<sub>r</sub> <sub>va</sub>lidit<sub>y</sub> i<sub>s</sub> h<sub>ar</sub>d t<sub>o es</sub>t<sub>a</sub>bli<sub>s</sub>h<sub>:</sub> th<sub>ey can pro</sub>d<sub>uce p</sub>l<sub>aus</sub>ibl<sub>e</sub> i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>erac</sub>ti<sub>ons w</sub>ith<sub>ou</sub>t <sub>repro</sub>d<sub>uc</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> th<sub>e ac</sub>t<sub>ors, ac</sub>ti<sub>ons, or ou</sub>t<sub>comes o</sub>f <sub>rea</sub>l <sub>po</sub>li<sub>cy</sub> processes. In practice they are judged by qualitative plausibility rather than realized outcomes (Larooij and Törnberg 2025; Barnett, Kieslich, and Diako<sub>p</sub>oulos 2024), and role-<sub>p</sub>la<sub>y</sub>ed a<sub>g</sub>ents over-conver<sub>g</sub>e (Chuan<sub>g</sub> et al. 2024), mistrack real <sub>g</sub>rou<sub>p</sub>s (Santurkar et al. 2023), and carr<sub>y p</sub>olitical biases (Rozado 2024).

The actor layer is the core of the benchmark. A forecast that stops at “will it pass” does not tell an analyst which stakeholders a measure moves, how each responds, or whether it is helped or harmed. GPS-Bench therefore evaluates three linked targets over one evidence-grounded graph: legislative passage, afected-actor identification, and actorlevel impact direction, the two actor-level targets being its spine. Other trajectory components (mechanisms, world state chan<sub>g</sub>es, multi-ste<sub>p</sub> ed<sub>g</sub>es) are emitted and structured but not <sub>score</sub>d<sub>, so we</sub> t<sub>es</sub>t <sub>on</sub>l<sub>y</sub> th<sub>e par</sub>t<sub>s w</sub>ith <sub>re</sub>t<sub>rospec</sub>ti<sub>ve</sub> l<sub>a</sub>b<sub>e</sub>l<sub>s</sub> (a<sub>pp</sub>endix).

We compare joint i<sub>n</sub>f<sub>erence over</sub> th<sub>e comp</sub>l<sub>e</sub>t<sub>e po</sub>li<sub>cy s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e w</sub>ith i<sub>n</sub>d<sub>epen</sub>d<sub>en</sub>t <sub>ac-</sub> t<sub>or</sub> i<sub>n</sub>f<sub>erence,</sub> <sub>s</sub>i<sub>ng</sub>l<sub>e-rev</sub>i<sub>s</sub>i<sub>on</sub> <sub>co</sub>ll<sub>a</sub>b<sub>ora</sub>ti<sub>on,</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>mu</sub>lti<sub>-roun</sub>d <sub>commun</sub>i<sub>ca</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub>

## Contributions.

• Benchmark. An evidence-<sub>g</sub>rounded tem<sub>p</sub>oral bench-<sub>mar</sub>k li<sub>n</sub>ki<sub>ng</sub> AI <sub>po</sub>li<sub>c</sub>i<sub>es</sub> t<sub>o</sub> d<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>an</sub>t<sub>ece</sub>d<sub>en</sub>t<sub>s, po</sub>li<sub>cy-</sub> <sub>spec</sub>ifi<sub>c ac</sub>t<sub>ors, recor</sub>d<sub>e</sub>d b<sub>e</sub>h<sub>av</sub>i<sub>our an</sub>d <sub>o</sub>b<sub>serve</sub>d <sub>s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>k<sub>e-</sub> h<sub>o</sub>ld<sub>er e</sub>f<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>s, w</sub>ith <sub>no</sub>d<sub>e- an</sub>d <sub>e</sub>d<sub>ge-</sub>l<sub>eve</sub>l <sub>provenance</sub> t<sub>o</sub> <sub>pu</sub>bli<sub>c recor</sub>d<sub>s.</sub>

• Representation. ## Related Work and Dataset Gap

LLM-based social and multi-agent simulation. Grounding and validity of simulated agents. We treat <sub>g</sub>roundin<sub>g</sub> <sub>no</sub>t <sub>as</sub> <sub>preprocess</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> b<sub>u</sub>t <sub>as</sub> <sub>a</sub> <sub>measure</sub>d <sub>con</sub>t<sub>r</sub>ib<sub>u</sub>ti<sub>on:</sub> <sub>ac</sub>t<sub>ors</sub> <sub>are recons</sub>t<sub>ruc</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d f<sub>rom</sub> th<sub>e pu</sub>bli<sub>c recor</sub>d <sub>an</sub>d <sub>score</sub>d <sub>aga</sub>i<sub>ns</sub>t b<sub>e</sub>h<sub>av</sub>i<sub>our</sub> th<sub>ey are</sub> d<sub>ocumen</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d t<sub>o</sub> h<sub>ave</sub> t<sub>a</sub>k<sub>en.</sub>

## Problem Formulation

GPS <sub>represen</sub>t<sub>s po</sub>li<sub>cy s</sub>i<sub>mu</sub>l<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on as</sub> th<sub>e con</sub>ti<sub>nua</sub>ti<sub>on o</sub>f<sub>a par-</sub> tiall<sub>y</sub> observed t<sub>yp</sub>ed tem<sub>p</sub>oral <sub>g</sub>ra<sub>p</sub>h (Fi<sub>g</sub>ure 1; node si<sub>g</sub>natures and the per-edge tuple in the appendix). Given $\mathrm { d o } ( P )$ <sub>, an</sub> i<sub>n</sub>f<sub>erence mec</sub>h<sub>an</sub>i<sub>sm pre</sub>di<sub>c</sub>t<sub>s a con</sub>ti<sub>nua</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> $\hat { G } ^ { * } ( b ) = \{ \hat { Y } , \hat { R } , \hat { A } , \hat { M } , \hat { W } , \hat { I } \}$ (<sub>p</sub>assa<sub>g</sub>e outcome, relevant actors, actions, mechanisms, world-state chan<sub>g</sub>es, im<sub>p</sub>acts); <sub>e</sub>d<sub>ges</sub> <sub>carry</sub> di<sub>rec</sub>ti<sub>on,</sub> <sub>pro</sub>b<sub>a</sub>bilit<sub>y,</sub> l<sub>ag,</sub> <sub>con</sub>t<sub>ro</sub>lli<sub>ng</sub> <sub>ac</sub>t<sub>or,</sub> <sub>an</sub>d provenance. Actors are drawn from a fixed roster of 34 stakeholder roles <sub>cover</sub>i<sub>ng every ac</sub>t<sub>or name</sub>d i<sub>n</sub> th<sub>e</sub> bill<sub>s</sub>’ <sub>causa</sub>l <sub>c</sub>h<sub>a</sub>i<sub>ns; per</sub> bill <sub>a</sub> <sub>cas</sub>t i<sub>s</sub> <sub>se</sub>l<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d b<sub>y</sub> <sub>scor</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> <sub>eac</sub>h <sub>ros</sub>t<sub>er</sub> <sub>ac</sub>t<sub>or</sub> f<sub>or</sub> <sub>re</sub>l<sub>e-</sub> <sub>vance</sub> <sub>aga</sub>i<sub>ns</sub>t th<sub>e</sub> <sub>ac</sub>t<sub>ors</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>recor</sub>d <sub>names,</sub> <sub>w</sub>h<sub>ere</sub> <sub>reca</sub>ll i<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e</sub> meaningful side because the label counts only named actors. Personas from the record. Each actor’s collected behavioural facts (roll-call and s<sub>p</sub>onsorshi<sub>p</sub> histor<sub>y</sub>, filin<sub>g</sub>s, lobb<sub>y</sub>in<sub>g</sub>, trade series) become <sub>p</sub>er-actor dated memories: <sub>a persona</sub> b<sub>u</sub>ilt f<sub>rom</sub> d<sub>ocumen</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d b<sub>e</sub>h<sub>av</sub>i<sub>our ra</sub>th<sub>er</sub> th<sub>an a</sub> stipulated objective. A model-written persona is not gold; it i<sub>s a</sub>d<sub>m</sub>i<sub>ss</sub>ibl<sub>e on</sub>l<sub>y as score</sub>d <sub>aga</sub>i<sub>ns</sub>t b<sub>e</sub>h<sub>av</sub>i<sub>our</sub> th<sub>e ac</sub>t<sub>or</sub> i<sub>s</sub> <sub>recor</sub>d<sub>e</sub>d <sub>as</sub> h<sub>av</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> t<sub>a</sub>k<sub>en,</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>sparse-recor</sub>d <sub>c</sub>l<sub>asses</sub> <sub>are</sub> <sub>re-</sub> <sub>por</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>ra</sub>th<sub>er</sub> th<sub>an</sub> <sub>average</sub>d <sub>away.</sub> P<sub>er-</sub>bill <sub>an</sub>t<sub>ece</sub>d<sub>en</sub>t <sub>even</sub>t<sub>s</sub> <sub>are</sub> <sub>assem</sub>bl<sub>e</sub>d <sub>un</sub>d<sub>er</sub> t<sub>wo</sub> <sub>ru</sub>l<sub>es,</sub> <sub>eac</sub>h d<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>s</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>c</sub>tl<sub>y</sub> b<sub>e</sub>f<sub>ore</sub> th<sub>e</sub> bill<sub>, an</sub>d th<sub>e</sub> bill’<sub>s own</sub> d<sub>owns</sub>t<sub>ream even</sub>t<sub>s w</sub>ithh<sub>e</sub>ld<sub>, w</sub>hi<sub>c</sub>h truncates what we inject but not the simulator’s pretraining, <sub>so a c</sub>l<sub>ean recor</sub>d i<sub>s necessary</sub> b<sub>u</sub>t <sub>no</sub>t <sub>su</sub>fi<sub>c</sub>i<sub>en</sub>t f<sub>or</sub> l<sub>ea</sub>k<sub>age</sub> control (a<sub>pp</sub>endix).

Impact and world state. Every actor–impact record is <sub>p</sub>rojected onto a second ontolo<sub>gy</sub>, actor → action → im<sub>p</sub>act, classifying each action into one of 12 mechanisms and each impact by world perspective (ten), causal depth (D1–D4) and functional role (twelve), all deterministic from the collected fields. Over the corpus this yields 402 typed impact nodes and 214 graded causal maps across 1,233 instruments. GPS <sub>separa</sub>t<sub>es</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>cons</sub>t<sub>ruc</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>o</sub>f th<sub>e</sub> <sub>o</sub>b<sub>serve</sub>d <sub>po</sub>li<sub>cy</sub> <sub>s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub> f<sub>rom</sub> th<sub>e mec</sub>h<sub>an</sub>i<sub>sm use</sub>d t<sub>o pre</sub>di<sub>c</sub>t it<sub>s con</sub>ti<sub>nua</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub> F<sub>or</sub> bill $b ,$ <sub>a</sub>ll i<sub>n</sub>f<sub>erence mo</sub>d<sub>es rece</sub>i<sub>ve</sub> th<sub>e same ev</sub>id<sub>ence-groun</sub>d<sub>e</sub>d g<sup>ra</sup>p<sup>h</sup> $G _ { < t } ( b )$ <sub>an</sub>d <sub>pro</sub>d<sub>uce</sub> <sub>pre</sub>di<sub>c</sub>ti<sub>ons</sub> i<sub>n</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>same</sub> <sub>ou</sub>t<sub>pu</sub>t <sub>sc</sub>h<sub>ema</sub> $\hat { G } ^ { * } \hat { ( b ) }$ <sub>, so</sub> i<sub>n</sub>f<sub>erence s</sub>t<sub>ra</sub>t<sub>eg</sub>i<sub>es can</sub> b<sub>e compare</sub>d <sub>w</sub>ith<sub>-</sub> <sub>ou</sub>t <sub>c</sub>h<sub>ang</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> th<sub>e un</sub>d<sub>er</sub>l<sub>y</sub>i<sub>ng represen</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on or</sub> th<sub>e ev</sub>id<sub>ence</sub> <sub>ava</sub>il<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> t<sub>o</sub> th<sub>e sys</sub>t<sub>em.</sub> W<sub>e eva</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>t<sub>e</sub> th<sub>ree mo</sub>d<sub>es</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t dif<sub>-</sub> f<sub>er on</sub>l<sub>y</sub> i<sub>n w</sub>h<sub>a</sub>t <sub>eac</sub>h LLM <sub>ca</sub>ll <sub>sees, no</sub>t i<sub>n</sub> th<sub>e ev</sub>id<sub>ence</sub> <sub>ava</sub>il<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e:</sub>

• Joint $f _ { \mathrm { j o i n t } } .$ : full bill + full cast + all actor states → all <sup>actor</sup> <sup>o</sup>u<sup>t</sup>pu<sup>ts</sup> <sup>in</sup> <sup>one</sup> p<sup>ass</sup>.

• Independent $f _ { \mathrm { i n d } } \colon$ th<sub>e same grap</sub>h <sub>par</sub>titi<sub>one</sub>d t<sub>o one ac-</sub> tor at a time → that actor’s out<sub>p</sub>ut, no cross-actor context.

Evidence-grounded actor states. Each agent is initialised f<sub>rom a</sub> d<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>ac</sub>t<sub>or s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub> d<sub>er</sub>i<sub>ve</sub>d f<sub>rom pu</sub>bli<sub>c recor</sub>d<sub>s:</sub> i<sub>ns</sub>ti<sub>-</sub> t<sub>u</sub>ti<sub>ona</sub>l <sub>ro</sub>l<sub>e,</sub> <sub>o</sub>b<sub>serve</sub>d <sub>pos</sub>iti<sub>ons,</sub> <sub>pr</sub>i<sub>or</sub> <sub>ac</sub>ti<sub>ons,</sub> <sub>resources,</sub> <sub>re</sub>l<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>ons</sub>hi<sub>ps an</sub>d <sub>re</sub>l<sub>evan</sub>t i<sub>n</sub>di<sub>ca</sub>t<sub>ors, eac</sub>h <sub>carry</sub>i<sub>ng source</sub> <sub>provenance an</sub>d <sub>res</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>c</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d t<sub>o</sub> i<sub>n</sub>f<sub>orma</sub>ti<sub>on ava</sub>il<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> b<sub>e</sub>f<sub>ore</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>s</sub>i<sub>mu</sub>l<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d d<sub>ec</sub>i<sub>s</sub>i<sub>on po</sub>i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>.</sub> A l<sub>ow-we</sub>i<sub>g</sub>ht <sub>scenar</sub>i<sub>o-pr</sub>i<sub>or</sub> l<sub>ayer</sub> from published forward-looking analyses (AI Futures Project 2025a,b) is evaluated se<sub>p</sub>aratel<sub>y</sub> and never used as a <sub>g</sub>roundtruth label. The state is realised in the prompt as a persona, <sub>w</sub>h<sub>ose recons</sub>t<sub>ruc</sub>ti<sub>on accuracy aga</sub>i<sub>ns</sub>t h<sub>e</sub>ld<sub>-ou</sub>t <sub>recor</sub>d<sub>s</sub> i<sub>s</sub> <sub>repor</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d i<sub>n</sub> th<sub>e</sub> R<sub>esu</sub>lt<sub>s.</sub>

Conditioning on prior measures. Each agent is additionally conditioned in context on labelled pre-2024 examples:

few-shot, our surrogate for fine-tuning the hosted 72B models, alongside a genuine weight-level LoRA of an open 7B model (Table 4), so <sub>p</sub>redictions are calibrated to how com<sub>p</sub>a-<sub>ra</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> <sub>ac</sub>t<sub>ors</sub> b<sub>e</sub>h<sub>ave</sub>d <sub>on</sub> <sub>compara</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> <sub>measures</sub> <sub>w</sub>ith<sub>ou</sub>t <sub>see</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> the ≥2024 test <sub>p</sub>eriod.

Interaction regimes. Over identical inputs the agents run in three regimes: independent (each answers from its own persona in isolation), collaborative (each sees a shared board of the others’ positions and revises once), and communication (over several rounds each agent chooses which others to convince or all<sub>y</sub> with, sends a messa<sub>g</sub>e, and u<sub>p</sub>dates): foll<sub>owe</sub>d b<sub>y a syn</sub>th<sub>es</sub>i<sub>s pass</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t <sub>rea</sub>d<sub>s a s</sub>i<sub>ng</sub>l<sub>e wor</sub>ld <sub>s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e o</sub>f th<sub>e</sub> t<sub>ranscr</sub>i<sub>p</sub>t<sub>.</sub> C<sub>ompar</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> th<sub>ese</sub> <sub>aga</sub>i<sub>ns</sub>t th<sub>e</sub> <sub>zero-s</sub>h<sub>o</sub>t <sub>s</sub>i<sub>ng</sub>l<sub>e</sub> <sub>ca</sub>ll l<sub>e</sub>t<sub>s</sub> <sub>us</sub> <sub>measure</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>con</sub>t<sub>r</sub>ib<sub>u</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>o</sub>f th<sub>e</sub> <sub>agen</sub>t <sub>mac</sub>hi<sub>nery</sub> rather than assume it (Results).

## C.1 Datasets and splits

Train/test split. Every forecasting claim uses a strict temporal split: train <2024, test ≥2024, not random (Table A4). The LLM predictors are temporally separated by model cutof (gpt-3.5-turbo-0613, trained to September 2021), <sub>so</sub> <sub>no</sub> LLM i<sub>s</sub> fitt<sub>e</sub>d <sub>an</sub>d “t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub>” <sub>mar</sub>k<sub>s</sub> <sub>on</sub>l<sub>y</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>exc</sub>l<sub>us</sub>i<sub>on</sub> boundary. The two fitted models are the weight-level LoRA fine-tune (main <sub>p</sub>a<sub>p</sub>er) and the <sub>g</sub>ra<sub>p</sub>h-structural im<sub>p</sub>act h<sub>y</sub>- b<sub>r</sub>id<sub>;</sub> th<sub>e</sub> l<sub>a</sub>tt<sub>er</sub> t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>ns on</sub> th<sub>e su</sub>b<sub>-</sub>2k <sub>pre-</sub>2024 <sub>pr</sub>i<sub>or an</sub>d i<sub>s a</sub> caveated, non-recommended baseline (Results).

Leakage-free passage. Leakage-freeness and label resol<sub>u</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>are</sub> i<sub>n</sub> t<sub>ens</sub>i<sub>on:</sub> <sub>a</sub> <sub>measure</sub> <sub>avo</sub>id<sub>s</sub> t<sub>empora</sub>l <sub>con</sub>t<sub>am</sub>i<sub>na-</sub> tion only because it is recent, but is labelledfailed only once its le<sub>g</sub>islature adjourns (u<sub>p</sub> to two <sub>y</sub>ears), whereas ado<sub>p</sub>tion is <sub>o</sub>b<sub>serva</sub>bl<sub>e a</sub>t <sub>once.</sub> P<sub>os</sub>t<sub>-cu</sub>t<sub>o</sub>f <sub>reso</sub>l<sub>ve</sub>d <sub>nega</sub>ti<sub>ves are</sub> th<sub>ere-</sub> f<sub>ore</sub> th<sub>e</sub> bi<sub>n</sub>di<sub>ng cons</sub>t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>, an</sub>d th<sub>e reac</sub>h<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e poo</sub>l <sub>con</sub>t<sub>a</sub>i<sub>ns</sub> onl<sub>y</sub> three (one vetoed state bill, two withdrawn international measures). Decision modes and prompts. Over identical inputs we compare three decision modes: zero-shot (one joint call), independent agents (each actor answers alone), and collaborative agents (each sees a shared board and revises, or communicates over several rounds): plus an in-context few-shot <sub>var</sub>i<sub>an</sub>t <sub>con</sub>diti<sub>one</sub>d <sub>on</sub> $k \in \{ 0 , 1 \bar { 6 } \}$ l<sub>a</sub>b<sub>e</sub>ll<sub>e</sub>d <sub>pre-</sub>2024 <sub>exam-</sub> <sub>p</sub>l<sub>es.</sub> V<sub>er</sub>b<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>m</sub> <sub>promp</sub>t t<sub>emp</sub>l<sub>a</sub>t<sub>es</sub> f<sub>or</sub> <sub>every</sub> t<sub>as</sub>k <sub>an</sub>d <sub>mo</sub>d<sub>e,</sub> k<sub>eye</sub>d t<sub>o</sub> th<sub>e</sub> bl<sub>oc</sub>k<sub>s o</sub>f T<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> A20<sub>, are</sub> i<sub>n</sub> A<sub>ppen</sub>di<sub>x</sub> L<sub>.</sub>

## C.4 Graph and non-LLM baselines

Th<sub>ree</sub> f<sub>am</sub>ili<sub>es o</sub>f <sub>me</sub>th<sub>o</sub>d <sub>consume</sub> thi<sub>s represen</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on, a</sub>ll trained (where trained at all) on <sub>p</sub>re-2024 measures and tested on ≥2024 (Table A20). (i) LLM: reads the typed context and <sub>p</sub>redicts the scored attributes directl<sub>y</sub> (relevant roles, welfare si<sub>g</sub>ns), trainin<sub>g</sub>-free or with in-context fewshot conditioning; the primary predictor. (ii) GNN, a onehop bill–actor message-passing network for relevance. (iii) Graph-structural prior, a rule reading the sign of typed ed<sub>g</sub>es (le<sub>g</sub>al-action <sub>p</sub>olarit<sub>y</sub> × variable valence × actor role), fused with the LLM <sub>g</sub>uess (the im<sub>p</sub>act h<sub>y</sub>brid). Families (ii)– (iii) are fitted on the thin sub-2k <sub>p</sub>re-2024 <sub>p</sub>rior and re<sub>p</sub>orted <sub>as cavea</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d b<sub>ase</sub>li<sub>nes;</sub> th<sub>e grap</sub>h’<sub>s pr</sub>i<sub>mary va</sub>l<sub>ue</sub> i<sub>s as</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>represen</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> th<sub>e</sub> LLM <sub>reasons over.</sub>

## D Experimental setup: fine-tuning, decoding, and tests

## D.1 Fine-tuning and decoding

Weight-level fine-tuning. Every LoRA arm uses one <sub>rec</sub>i<sub>pe, var</sub>i<sub>e</sub>d <sub>on</sub>l<sub>y</sub> i<sub>n</sub> it<sub>s</sub> t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> fil<sub>e.</sub> B<sub>ase mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct; LoRA rank 16, α=32, dropout 0.05, adapters on all attention and MLP projections $( \mathrm { q } , \mathrm { k } , \mathrm { v } , \mathrm { o } , \mathrm { g a t e } , \mathrm { u p } , \mathrm { d o w n \_ p r o j } )$ , 40.4M trainable parameters (0.53% of 7.7B). Training is bf16 with gradient checkpointing, per-device batch size 1 and gradient accumulation 8 (efective batch 8), AdamW at learning rate $2 \times 1 0 ^ { - 4 }$ cosine schedule with warmup ratio 0.03, three epochs, maximum sequence length 2,048 tokens. Loss is computed on th<sub>e ass</sub>i<sub>s</sub>t<sub>an</sub>t <sub>comp</sub>l<sub>e</sub>ti<sub>on on</sub>l<sub>y: promp</sub>t t<sub>o</sub>k<sub>ens are mas</sub>k<sub>e</sub>d t<sub>o</sub> −100, and over-length examples are left-truncated so the an-<sub>swer an</sub>d th<sub>e genera</sub>ti<sub>on promp</sub>t <sub>surv</sub>i<sub>ve.</sub> H<sub>ar</sub>d<sub>ware</sub> i<sub>s</sub> t<sub>wo</sub> NVIDIA RTX 3090 cards (24 GB each) in one workstation with a 20-core Intel i7-12700K and 62 GB of RAM; one adapter trains on one card, and the k-fold arms run two folds concurrentl<sub>y</sub>. The host runs Debian 13 (trixie), kernel 6.12.73, with NVIDIA driver 595.58.03; training runs in a Conda environment on Python 3.12.13 with torch 2.12.0 (CUDA 13.0), transformers 5.9.0 and peft 0.19.1. A small arm (∼100 rows) trains in under a minute, the lar<sub>g</sub>est (∼2,200 rows) in about fifteen.

Decoding. All local evaluation is greedy (do\_sample=False), so the onl<sub>y</sub> stochasticit<sub>y</sub> in a fi<sub>ne-</sub>t<sub>une</sub>d <sub>arm</sub> i<sub>s</sub> t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng.</sub> G<sub>enera</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> b<sub>u</sub>d<sub>ge</sub>t<sub>s are</sub> th<sub>e</sub> smallest that fit the answer schema: 16 new tokens for impact direction, 24 for the k-fold action verb, 80 for the multilabel action list, 90 for the multi-agent turns. Hosted models are called at temperature 0 through a single API layer; note that this does not make them deterministic; b<sub>y</sub>t<sub>e-</sub>id<sub>en</sub>ti<sub>ca</sub>l <sub>reruns</sub> di<sub>sagree on a</sub> fifth <sub>o</sub>f <sub>pre</sub>di<sub>c</sub>ti<sub>ons on</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>sma</sub>ll<sub>es</sub>t t<sub>es</sub>t<sub>.</sub>

## H.2 Analysis: mechanisms behind the results

The preceding section reports what each mode predicts; here we explain why, through the interaction structure the agents <sub>expose,</sub> th<sub>e groun</sub>di<sub>ng</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t d<sub>r</sub>i<sub>ves ac</sub>t<sub>or pre</sub>di<sub>c</sub>ti<sub>on, an</sub>d <sub>a</sub> <sub>qua</sub>lit<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>ve case.</sub>

Coalition formation. To characterise what communication does, we instrument the convince/all<sub>y</sub> ste<sub>p</sub> (Fi<sub>g</sub>ure A6). Across 4,254 pitches from the two open arms, an agent almost always selects a target (1–4% pitch to none), and 77% of targets already hold the same stance as the source (homophily 0.77 pooled, 0.70 on DeepSeek-V3 and 0.84 on Qwen-72B): a<sub>g</sub>ents predominantl<sub>y</sub> reinforce same-stance <sub>coa</sub>liti<sub>ons ra</sub>th<sub>er</sub> th<sub>an conver</sub>t <sub>opponen</sub>t<sub>s.</sub> Th<sub>e</sub> h<sub>eav</sub>i<sub>es</sub>t <sub>e</sub>d<sub>ges</sub> form a <sub>g</sub>overnment/re<sub>g</sub>ulator<sub>y</sub> core (state le<sub>g</sub>islatures ↔ re<sub>g</sub>- ulators) and an industr<sub>y</sub> bloc (business ↔ startu<sub>p</sub>s). This <sub>coa</sub>liti<sub>on-re</sub>i<sub>n</sub>f<sub>orc</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> t<sub>opo</sub>l<sub>ogy</sub> i<sub>s cons</sub>i<sub>s</sub>t<sub>en</sub>t <sub>w</sub>ith th<sub>e a</sub>b<sub>sence</sub> <sub>o</sub>f <sub>accuracy ga</sub>i<sub>ns</sub> f<sub>rom a</sub>dditi<sub>ona</sub>l <sub>commun</sub>i<sub>ca</sub>ti<sub>on roun</sub>d<sub>s.</sub>

Case study: semiconductor export controls. We instantiate 13 evidence-grounded actors associated with the 2022 U<sub>.</sub>S<sub>. sem</sub>i<sub>con</sub>d<sub>uc</sub>t<sub>or expor</sub>t <sub>con</sub>t<sub>ro</sub>l<sub>s</sub> t<sub>o exam</sub>i<sub>ne w</sub>h<sub>e</sub>th<sub>er</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>commun</sub>i<sub>ca</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> t<sub>opo</sub>l<sub>ogy</sub> <sub>o</sub>b<sub>serve</sub>d <sub>a</sub>t <sub>sca</sub>l<sub>e</sub> <sub>a</sub>l<sub>so</sub> <sub>appears</sub> i<sub>n a po</sub>li<sub>cy-spec</sub>ifi<sub>c s</sub>i<sub>mu</sub>l<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub> A<sub>cross</sub> b<sub>o</sub>th <sub>open mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l<sub>s,</sub> t<sub>en commun</sub>i<sub>ca</sub>ti<sub>on roun</sub>d<sub>s pro</sub>d<sub>uce s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e coa</sub>liti<sub>on s</sub>t<sub>ruc-</sub> ture with limited stance conversion (Fi<sub>g</sub>ure A7). Joint-action <sub>s</sub>i<sub>mu</sub>l<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on s</sub>i<sub>m</sub>il<sub>ar</sub>l<sub>y</sub> f<sub>avours w</sub>ithi<sub>n-</sub>bl<sub>oc coor</sub>di<sub>na</sub>ti<sub>on un</sub>l<sub>ess</sub> <sub>cross-</sub>bl<sub>oc</sub> i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>erac</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> i<sub>s</sub> <sub>exp</sub>li<sub>c</sub>itl<sub>y</sub> <sub>requ</sub>i<sub>re</sub>d<sub>.</sub> Thi<sub>s</sub> <sub>case</sub> ill<sub>us-</sub> t<sub>ra</sub>t<sub>es</sub> h<sub>ow ac</sub>t<sub>or-</sub>d<sub>ecompose</sub>d i<sub>n</sub>f<sub>erence prov</sub>id<sub>es</sub> i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>erac</sub>ti<sub>on-</sub> l<sub>eve</sub>l <sub>ou</sub>t<sub>pu</sub>t<sub>s</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t <sub>comp</sub>l<sub>emen</sub>t<sub>,</sub> <sub>ra</sub>th<sub>er</sub> th<sub>an</sub> <sub>rep</sub>l<sub>ace,</sub> th<sub>e</sub> stronger aggregate predictions from joint inference; the det<sub>a</sub>il<sub>e</sub>d <sub>coa</sub>liti<sub>on ana</sub>l<sub>ys</sub>i<sub>s</sub> i<sub>s</sub> i<sub>n</sub> A<sub>ppen</sub>di<sub>x</sub> I<sub>.</sub>

When they can act, not just argue, coalitions still stop at the bloc line. ## K Worked example: one bill through each inference mode

Thi<sub>s appen</sub>di<sub>x wa</sub>lk<sub>s a rev</sub>i<sub>ewer, s</sub>t<sub>ep</sub> b<sub>y s</sub>t<sub>ep,</sub> th<sub>roug</sub>h <sub>w</sub>h<sub>a</sub>t GPS produces for a single input bill under each infer-<sub>ence mo</sub>d<sub>e, so</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t th<sub>e a</sub>b<sub>s</sub>t<sub>rac</sub>t <sub>sc</sub>h<sub>ema o</sub>f th<sub>e ma</sub>i<sub>n pa-</sub> <sub>per</sub> b<sub>ecomes concre</sub>t<sub>e.</sub> W<sub>e use a rea</sub>l <sub>run o</sub>f th<sub>e recor</sub>d<sub>-</sub> grounded actor system on the 2022 U.S. advanced-chip export controls (BIS, 7 Oct 2022; bar advanced AI chi<sub>p</sub>s and the tools to make them from China), with ten <sub>ev</sub>id<sub>ence-groun</sub>d<sub>e</sub>d <sub>ac</sub>t<sub>ors.</sub> E<sub>very s</sub>t<sub>ance, p</sub>it<sub>c</sub>h<sub>, an</sub>d <sub>move-</sub> <sub>men</sub>t <sub>coun</sub>t b<sub>e</sub>l<sub>ow</sub> i<sub>s</sub> t<sub>a</sub>k<sub>en</sub> <sub>ver</sub>b<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>m</sub> f<sub>rom</sub> th<sub>e</sub> l<sub>ogge</sub>d t<sub>ran-</sub> scri<sub>p</sub>t (sim\_uschina\_deliberation.json); nothi<sub>ng</sub> h<sub>ere</sub> i<sub>s</sub> ill<sub>us</sub>t<sub>ra</sub>ti<sub>ve-on</sub>l<sub>y.</sub>

Step 0: Input. The system receives the outcome-scrubbed bill context ⟨ctx⟩ and the evidence-grounded graph $G _ { \leq t } ( b )$ the ten actors, each with a dated state (role, <sub>p</sub>rior <sub>p</sub>ositions, resources, relationshi<sub>p</sub>s) restricted to information available before 7 Oct 2022. All three modes below see this same graph; they difer only in what each LLM call is shown. The required output schema is, for every actor, a response (the action it takes) and the welfare direction of the bill’s impact on it (+ benefits / − harms), which together populate $\hat { G } ^ { * } ( b )$

Step 1: Independent mode $( f _ { \mathrm { i n d } } )$ . Each actor is queried alone from its own persona, with no sight of any other actor. ## L Prompts and context per evaluation mode

E<sub>ac</sub>h <sub>paragrap</sub>h b<sub>e</sub>l<sub>ow g</sub>i<sub>ves</sub> th<sub>e exac</sub>t <sub>promp</sub>t f<sub>or one se</sub>tti<sub>ng</sub> of the consolidated results table (Table A20), ke<sub>y</sub>ed to its block. All settings share the same outcome-scrubbed context: the predictor sees only the bill name and summary, with outcome tells (enacted, vetoed, signed into law, . . . ) scrubbed, and not the ex<sub>p</sub>ert anal<sub>y</sub>sis, the <sub>g</sub>old label, or the evidence quote. We write ⟨ctx⟩ for that context and ⟨roster⟩ for the 34 actor labels. The default identity persona is onl<sub>y</sub> the actor’s identit<sub>y</sub>: “You ARE "{actor}", an actor in AI governance, reasoning only from your own interests.”, and the rich persona of block C re<sub>p</sub>laces it (defined below). No settin<sub>g</sub> is <sub>g</sub>iven <sub>any ac</sub>t<sub>or</sub>’<sub>s go</sub>ld di<sub>rec</sub>ti<sub>on or re</sub>l<sub>evance.</sub> Th<sub>e</sub> t<sub>emp</sub>l<sub>a</sub>t<sub>es are</sub> verbatim skeletons (JSON re<sub>p</sub>l<sub>y</sub> formats abbreviated).

## Block A: Zero-shot (one joint call).

• Relevance: “A neutral policy analyst   
reviews this AI bill: ⟨ctx⟩. Which of   
these actors are MATERIALLY affected   
(bear costs, gain/lose, must change   
behaviour)? Actors: ⟨roster⟩. JSON:   
{"related":[...]}”

• Impact direction: “. . .For each actor, is the   
impact BENEFICIAL(+) or HARMFUL(−)   
to it? Actors: ⟨gold-related actors⟩.   
JSON: {"dir":{"<actor>":"+/−"}}”

## Block A: Independent agents (each actor answers alone, no communication).

• Relevance: “⟨persona⟩ A bill is proposed:   
⟨ctx⟩. Are YOU materially affected?   
JSON: {"affected": true/false}”

• Impact: “⟨persona⟩ Bill: ⟨ctx⟩. Does   
this bill BENEFIT(+) you (funding,   
market, protection, lighter burden)   
or HARM(−) you (cost, liability,   
lost access, restriction)? JSON:   
{"dir":"+/−"}”

Block A: Collaborative, single revision (each actor sees the board once, then revises). Round 1 is the independent call above. Round 2: “⟨persona⟩ Bill: ⟨ctx⟩. Other parties’ first read: ⟨board of all actors’ round-1 answers⟩. In light of the others, [are YOU affected? / your sign?] JSON: {. . .}” (Blocks C and D also run this <sub>s</sub>i<sub>ng</sub>l<sub>e-rev</sub>i<sub>s</sub>i<sub>on co</sub>ll<sub>a</sub>b<sub>ora</sub>ti<sub>ve ca</sub>ll<sub>, w</sub>ith th<sub>e r</sub>i<sub>c</sub>h <sub>persona an</sub>d the antecedent block res<sub>p</sub>ectivel<sub>y</sub>.)

Collaborative, 3-round communication (convince/ally, then update; Table A20 and Figures A5–A6). Shared independent init, then three rounds of two steps each. Convince/ally: “⟨persona⟩ Bill: ⟨ctx⟩. Positions: ⟨board⟩. You want others to adopt your reading and may ally. Whom do you CONVINCE/ALLY with, and what do you argue? JSON: {"targets":[...],"message":"<one sentence>"}” Update: “⟨persona⟩ Bill: ⟨ctx⟩. Others’ positions: ⟨board⟩. Messages to you: ⟨inbox⟩. Weighing their arguments/alliances, your UPDATED sign. JSON: {. . .}” Only the messages actors send each other <sub>carry</sub> i<sub>n</sub>fl<sub>uence; no ex</sub>t<sub>erna</sub>l i<sub>n</sub>f<sub>orma</sub>ti<sub>on en</sub>t<sub>ers, so</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>reg</sub>i<sub>me</sub> i<sub>so</sub>l<sub>a</sub>t<sub>es</sub> th<sub>e e</sub>f<sub>ec</sub>t <sub>o</sub>f <sub>commun</sub>i<sub>ca</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> it<sub>se</sub>lf<sub>.</sub>

Block B: Few-shot (in-context conditioning on the training data; also Table A20). The block-A impact <sub>promp</sub>t <sub>o</sub>f th<sub>e</sub> <sub>c</sub>h<sub>osen</sub> <sub>mo</sub>d<sub>e,</sub> <sub>pre</sub>fi<sub>xe</sub>d <sub>w</sub>ith <sub>a</sub> l<sub>a</sub>b<sub>e</sub>ll<sub>e</sub>d<sub>-</sub> exam<sub>p</sub>le block: “Labelled examples (actor, bill → welfare impact on that actor): ⟨K balanced examples drawn from the pre-2024 train split, each - actor: {a}; bill: {text} -> +/−⟩” followed b<sub>y</sub> the <sub>usua</sub>l <sub>per-ac</sub>t<sub>or</sub> i<sub>mpac</sub>t <sub>ques</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub> E<sub>very examp</sub>l<sub>e</sub> i<sub>s</sub> d<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d before 2024 and the test bill is ≥2024 and bill-disjoint from th<sub>e</sub> <sub>examp</sub>l<sub>es,</sub> <sub>so</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>con</sub>diti<sub>on</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> i<sub>s</sub> t<sub>empora</sub>ll<sub>y</sub> <sub>separa</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d<sub>.</sub> Reported at k=0 (no examples) and k=16.

Th<sub>e</sub> r<sub>e</sub>m<sub>a</sub>inin<sub>g</sub> <sub>pa</sub>r<sub>ag</sub>r<sub>ap</sub>h<sub>s</sub> <sub>g</sub>i<sub>ve</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>p</sub>r<sub>o</sub>m<sub>p</sub>t<sub>s</sub> f<sub>o</sub>r th<sub>e</sub> t<sub>a</sub>r<sub>ge</sub>t<sub>s</sub> and models be<sub>y</sub>ond Table A20: the multilabel action task (Tables A7, A8), the wei<sub>g</sub>ht-level LoRA fine-tune (main <sub>p</sub>a<sub>p</sub>er), im<sub>p</sub>act via an inde<sub>p</sub>endent jud<sub>g</sub>e (Table A9), title-onl<sub>y</sub> <sub>p</sub>assa<sub>g</sub>e, and the full-cor<sub>p</sub>us relevance/im<sub>p</sub>act <sub>p</sub>rior (Table A6). Actor-action, multilabel with explicit negatives (Table A7). Per (bill, actor), over the 15-action vocabulary ⟨vocab⟩: “⟨persona⟩⟨history⟩ Bill: ⟨ctx⟩. List EVERY public action you take in response to this bill (zero or more). Choose only from: ⟨vocab⟩. If you take no public action, return an empty list. JSON: {"actions":[...]}” The em<sub>p</sub>t<sub>y</sub>-list o<sub>p</sub>tion is what makes the verified-no-action negatives scorable. ⟨history⟩ is empty in the base row and, when conditioned, is <sub>one o</sub>f th<sub>e</sub> th<sub>ree</sub> f<sub>orms</sub> b<sub>e</sub>l<sub>ow.</sub>

Conditioning forms for the action target (Table A8). All three are temporally restricted to bills dated before the target. Action repertoire (flat track record): “Your documented track record on earlier bills (most-recent first): ⟨(year) title: action-types; . . .⟩. Across your record your actions break down as: ⟨distribution⟩.” Structural rationale: <sub>eac</sub>h <sub>pr</sub>i<sub>or ac</sub>ti<sub>on w</sub>ith th<sub>e wor</sub>ld<sub>-s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e var</sub>i<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> it t<sub>arge</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d<sub>,</sub> it<sub>s</sub> <sub>we</sub>lf<sub>are</sub> di<sub>rec</sub>ti<sub>on,</sub> <sub>an</sub>d th<sub>e</sub> <sub>co-ac</sub>t<sub>ors:</sub> “({y}) {title}: you {action} [{type}] targeting {variable} ({dir} to you) amid {other actors}.” Stated motive (the actor’s <sub>own-vo</sub>i<sub>ce</sub> <sub>reason</sub> <sub>per</sub> <sub>pr</sub>i<sub>or</sub> bill<sub>,</sub> <sub>wr</sub>itt<sub>en</sub> b<sub>y</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>separa</sub>t<sub>e</sub> <sub>an-</sub> notator <sub>g</sub>rounded in the record, NONE where unsu<sub>pp</sub>orted): “({y}) {title}: {action-types} - Because ⟨stated motive⟩.”

Weight-level LoRA fine-tune (main paper). The fine-tuned model uses the same prompts as its in-context counterpart; only the weights difer. Relevance (one joint call per bill over the 12 functional roles ⟨menu⟩): “For this bill, which stakeholder roles does it MATERIALLY affect? Bill: ⟨ctx⟩. Choose any number from these roles: ⟨menu⟩. Table A5: All GPS-Bench prediction results in one table (blocks A–D on the 34-role rounded cor us, 153 ost-2024 test bills except D’s 17; block E on the earlier 18-role corpus). The three model columns are <sup>∗</sup>GPT-3.5 (temporally separated, Se<sub>p</sub>t-2021 cutof), Dee<sub>p</sub>Seek and Qwen (≥2024-cutof); block E swa<sub>p</sub>s Qwen for Claude Sonnet-4.5, a leak<sub>y</sub> u<sub>pp</sub>er bound (see its header). The two trained-baseline columns are the <sub>p</sub>a<sub>p</sub>er’s non-LLM <sub>p</sub>redictor families, each a sin<sub>g</sub>le fitted confi<sub>g</sub>uration with no decision modes (sub-2k prior, caveated): GNN, our one-hop bill–actor relevance model (block A F1 0.30; block E F1 0.58), and Hybrid, the ra h-structural im act-direction model, the bill’s le al-action olarit crossed with the measurable variable’s valence, fused with a DeepSeek per-pair guess via a gradient-boosted tree (block A impact F1 0.76). Each sits on its one task’s row; every other cell is “: ”. Reading the blocks: (A) joint zero-shot wins relevance and impact on every model, the multi-agent modes win nothing, and the trained baselines trail the best LLM (GNN 0.30 on relevance; Hybrid 0.76 on impact vs. GPT-3.5 0.77, though it edges out its DeepSeek base 0.71); (B) few-shot conditioning lifts every model and mode, most in the multi-a<sub>g</sub>ent cells; (C) a rich <sub>p</sub>ersona alone is a <sub>g</sub>amble, it hel<sub>p</sub>s GPT-3.5 and Qwen but crashes Dee<sub>p</sub>Seek, while rich+few-shot is reliabl<sub>y</sub> stron<sub>g</sub> on all three; (D) antecedents do not hel<sub>p</sub> im<sub>p</sub>act and hurt most under collaboration on the o<sub>p</sub>en models; (E) on the 18-role corpus zero-shot again wins both tasks and the trained GNN (F1 0.58) trails ever<sub>y</sub> LLM; (F) the same joint>independent>collaborative ordering reproduces on the human-curated release named-actor pool (DeepSeek-V3 0.71/0.62/0.58), and when the collaborative agents may commit joint actions they form 42 record-grounded coalitions across the four richest bills (co-si<sub>g</sub>ned frameworks, shared funds), the interaction structure a sin<sub>g</sub>le <sub>p</sub>rediction does not ex<sub>p</sub>ress. Rel. F1 is relevant-class F1: recall-dominated, since only im<sub>p</sub>act-recorded roles are annotated <sub>p</sub>ositive (Dee<sub>p</sub>Seek recall 0.69/0.52, Qwen 0.90/0.79 for none/fine-tuned). Imp. F1 is macro-F1 of the welfare sign, Imp. acc its accuracy. Fine-tuned = in-context conditioning on a bill-specific prior: th<sub>e re</sub>l<sub>evan</sub>t <sub>ac</sub>t<sub>ors an</sub>d th<sub>e</sub>i<sub>r we</sub>lf<sub>are s</sub>i<sub>gns</sub> d<sub>rawn</sub> f<sub>rom eac</sub>h bill’s nearest <sub>p</sub>rior bills, retrieved b<sub>y</sub> TF-IDF similarit<sub>y</sub> (<sub>y</sub>ear < target). Passage from the title alone sits near the majority baseline (0.50), consistent with the jurisdiction confound; <sub>re</sub>l<sub>evance an</sub>d i<sub>mpac</sub>t <sub>are we</sub>ll <sub>a</sub>b<sub>ove</sub> th<sub>e</sub>i<sub>r</sub> t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>v</sub>i<sub>a</sub>l b<sub>ase</sub>li<sub>nes.</sub> O<sub>pen-we</sub>i<sub>g</sub>ht<sub>s pre</sub>di<sub>c</sub>t<sub>ors on</sub>l<sub>y.</sub>

## M Data-to-Agent Validation Matrix

The benchmark’s organizing principle: every simulated actor has a real-data source and an independent validator; every bill a real database entry with an oficial status; every antecedent event a dated public document, so an actor’s state is derivedfrom observable data and scored against its historical actions, not prompted. where x is the source excerpt, A is the required actor subset set, g is the governance gap, m is capability or hazard movement, c is the failed control, e is the endpoint, p is the pathway, z is the closure variable, b is the bottleneck, and r i<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>repa</sub>i<sub>r.</sub>

## N.2 Problem Model: Actors, Pathways, and Closure

Actor Subsets A governance measure is complete only if th<sub>e</sub> <sub>ac</sub>t<sub>or</sub> <sub>su</sub>b<sub>se</sub>t<sub>s</sub> <sub>requ</sub>i<sub>re</sub>d f<sub>or</sub> i<sub>mp</sub>l<sub>emen</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on,</sub> <sub>ver</sub>ifi<sub>ca-</sub> ti<sub>on, an</sub>d <sub>en</sub>f<sub>orcemen</sub>t <sub>are covere</sub>d<sub>.</sub> W<sub>e use</sub> t<sub>en ro</sub>l<sub>e-</sub>l<sub>eve</sub>l actor subsets (frontier develo<sub>p</sub>ers, com<sub>p</sub>ute <sub>p</sub>roviders, re<sub>g</sub>- <sub>u</sub>l<sub>a</sub>t<sub>ors, eva</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>t<sub>ors, mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l h<sub>u</sub>b<sub>s, open-we</sub>i<sub>g</sub>ht <sub>ac</sub>t<sub>ors,</sub> f<sub>or-</sub> <sub>e</sub>i<sub>gn s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>t<sub>es,</sub> d<sub>ep</sub>l<sub>oyers, an</sub>d <sub>cr</sub>iti<sub>ca</sub>l<sub>-</sub>i<sub>n</sub>f<sub>ras</sub>t<sub>ruc</sub>t<sub>ure opera-</sub> tors/afected <sub>p</sub>ublics), ma<sub>pp</sub>ed b<sub>y</sub> the <sub>g</sub>overnance lever an actor controls, not b<sub>y</sub> identit<sub>y</sub> (an actor can belon<sub>g</sub> to several).

Failure Pathways Start with the retraining levy. Firms subject to it can shift the work to contractors abroad, so the levied activity slips outside the rule’s reach, a coverage failure (foreign free-riding when the decisive actor is ofshore); and if the obli<sub>g</sub>ation carries no real <sub>p</sub>enalt<sub>y</sub>, the levy is an enforcement gap. These are two of seven recurrin<sub>g g</sub>overnance-failure mechanisms (forei<sub>g</sub>n free-ridin<sub>g</sub>, enf<sub>orcemen</sub>t <sub>gap,</sub> <sub>capa</sub>bilit<sub>y</sub> l<sub>ea</sub>k<sub>age,</sub> <sub>open-we</sub>i<sub>g</sub>ht <sub>pro</sub>lif<sub>era</sub>ti<sub>on,</sub> <sub>g</sub>overnance la<sub>g</sub>, rivalr<sub>y</sub> acceleration, s<sub>y</sub>stemic de<sub>p</sub>endenc<sub>y</sub>), <sub>eac</sub>h <sub>a</sub> <sub>c</sub>h<sub>a</sub>i<sub>n</sub> <sub>o</sub>f <sub>rea</sub>l <sub>even</sub>t<sub>s</sub> <sub>esca</sub>l<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>ng</sub> t<sub>o</sub> <sub>a</sub> <sub>ca</sub>t<sub>as</sub>t<sub>rop</sub>hi<sub>c</sub> <sub>en</sub>d<sub>-</sub> <sub>p</sub>oint if left unclosed (Table A32). A side exam<sub>p</sub>le shows the <sub>range:</sub> <sub>a</sub> f<sub>ron</sub>ti<sub>er</sub> t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> li<sub>cense</sub> bi<sub>n</sub>d<sub>s</sub> d<sub>omes</sub>ti<sub>c</sub> l<sub>a</sub>b<sub>s,</sub> b<sub>u</sub>t <sub>an</sub> i<sub>ns</sub>id<sub>er can move mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l <sub>we</sub>i<sub>g</sub>ht<sub>s a</sub>b<sub>roa</sub>d<sub>, unreca</sub>ll<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e once</sub> gone, capability leakage, the containment failure from the A.Q. Khan network (2004) to a 2024 AI trade-secret theft. For the lev<sub>y</sub> the links whose re<sub>p</sub>air hel<sub>p</sub>s most are coverage and enforcement, so the fix is to redesign th<sub>e</sub> i<sub>ns</sub>t<sub>rumen</sub>t<sub>, a</sub> f<sub>un</sub>d<sub>e</sub>d t<sub>rans</sub>iti<sub>on</sub> f<sub>un</sub>d <sub>w</sub>ith <sub>man</sub>d<sub>a</sub>t<sub>ory</sub> di<sub>s-</sub> <sub>p</sub>l<sub>acemen</sub>t <sub>repor</sub>ti<sub>ng an</sub>d t<sub>ax</sub> i<sub>ncen</sub>ti<sub>ves, w</sub>hi<sub>c</sub>h fi<sub>rms canno</sub>t <sub>o</sub>f<sub>s</sub>h<sub>ore aroun</sub>d <sub>an</sub>d <sub>w</sub>hi<sub>c</sub>h <sub>rou</sub>t<sub>es suppor</sub>t t<sub>o</sub> th<sub>e wor</sub>k<sub>ers a</sub>t <sub>r</sub>i<sub>s</sub>k<sub>, exac</sub>tl<sub>y w</sub>h<sub>a</sub>t th<sub>e</sub> b<sub>r</sub>i<sub>e</sub>f <sub>recommen</sub>d<sub>s.</sub>

## N.4 How We Evaluate GPS

“I<sub>s</sub> GPS <sub>goo</sub>d?” h<sub>as one s</sub>t<sub>ruc</sub>t<sub>ure</sub>d <sub>answer:</sub> GPS i<sub>s a p</sub>i<sub>pe</sub>li<sub>ne</sub> <sub>w</sub>h<sub>ose</sub> <sub>every</sub> <sub>s</sub>t<sub>age</sub> i<sub>s</sub> <sub>a</sub> <sub>concre</sub>t<sub>e</sub> d<sub>ec</sub>i<sub>s</sub>i<sub>on</sub> <sub>ques</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>w</sub>ith it<sub>s</sub> own test and data (Table A18), each mapping to one of four validity dimensions, predictive, diagnostic, decision, and simulation. More broadly, every agent and transition has a real-data source and an independent validator, the historical record, not LLM jud<sub>g</sub>ment, as the label (A<sub>pp</sub>endix M); this <sub>p</sub>il<sub>o</sub>t <sub>va</sub>lid<sub>a</sub>t<sub>es</sub> th<sub>e</sub> d<sub>ec</sub>i<sub>s</sub>i<sub>on-cr</sub>iti<sub>ca</sub>l <sub>rows an</sub>d <sub>re</sub>l<sub>eases</sub> th<sub>e res</sub>t <sub>as</sub> th<sub>e</sub> b<sub>enc</sub>h<sub>mar</sub>k<sub>.</sub> Th<sub>roug</sub>h<sub>ou</sub>t <sub>we repor</sub>t Wil<sub>son</sub> i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>erva</sub>l<sub>s</sub> and treat small-n checks as plausibility signals.

Perceive, model, decide, a pipeline, not a single answer. A <sub>recommen</sub>d<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> i<sub>s on</sub>l<sub>y as soun</sub>d <sub>as</sub> th<sub>e wor</sub>ld GPS <sub>s</sub>t<sub>ar</sub>t<sub>s</sub> from, so we evaluate every stage, from perceiving the actors, through modeling the stakeholders and causal chain, to the decision, both in isolation and end-to-end, with all metrics <sub>co</sub>ll<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d i<sub>n</sub> T<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> A18<sub>.</sub> B<sub>ecause s</sub>t<sub>ages</sub> d<sub>epen</sub>d <sub>on eac</sub>h <sub>o</sub>th<sub>er,</sub> we do not multiply their accuracies (errors correlate; later stages recover); instead a sensitivity analysis (stable in 98.5% of ±0.10 trials) flags the 2/15 flip-prone decisions for human review. GPS also attaches a confidence to each answer from its validation strength, adoption and hindcast high, pathway medium, repair advisory, so an upstream error is surfaced, not silentl<sub>y p</sub>ro<sub>p</sub>a<sub>g</sub>ated (A<sub>pp</sub>endix N.5).

Strict Edge-Backed Chain Certification (SECC), Diagnostic The component does not assume a full policy-failure <sub>c</sub>h<sub>a</sub>i<sub>n can</sub> b<sub>e va</sub>lid<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>au</sub>t<sub>oma</sub>ti<sub>ca</sub>ll<sub>y en</sub>d<sub>-</sub>t<sub>o-en</sub>d<sub>;</sub> it d<sub>ecom-</sub> <sub>poses</sub> th<sub>e</sub> di<sub>agnos</sub>i<sub>s</sub> i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>o</sub> fi<sub>ve e</sub>d<sub>ges an</sub>d <sub>cer</sub>tifi<sub>es w</sub>hi<sub>c</sub>h <sub>sur-</sub> vive skeptical scrutiny. We keep a deliberately strict criterion: a chain is certified only if all five edges, policy gap → movement, movement → failed control, control → end<sub>p</sub>oint, end<sub>p</sub>oint → <sub>p</sub>athwa<sub>y</sub>, <sub>p</sub>athwa<sub>y</sub> → bottleneck, withstand an adversarial judge that tries to refute each and defaults to “refuted” when evidence is insuficient. A first pass judging bare chain steps certifies 0/57: the early edges are robust but the inter<sub>p</sub>retive tail is weak (Table A24), the final ed<sub>g</sub>es were left i<sub>mp</sub>li<sub>c</sub>it<sub>.</sub>

We therefore introduce Strict Edge-Backed Chain Certification (SECC), which makes each edge’s evidence explicit (a verbatim excer<sub>p</sub>t, the codebook warrant, a stated alternati<sub>ve,</sub> <sub>an</sub>d f<sub>or</sub> th<sub>e</sub> b<sub>o</sub>ttl<sub>enec</sub>k <sub>a</sub> t<sub>arge</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d<sub>-versus-wrong</sub> <sub>repa</sub>i<sub>r</sub> contrast) before the same all-five-ed<sub>g</sub>es test. Certification rises to 27/57 (0.47 [0.35, 0.60]; Table A24), but this measures whether each edge is defensible once its warrant is explicit, not blind survival. We therefore also run the harder test: a blind, cross-model re-certification. A diferent model (claude-opus-4-8; chains coded with claude-sonnet-4-6), <sub>g</sub>i<sub>ven on</sub>l<sub>y</sub> th<sub>e c</sub>h<sub>a</sub>i<sub>n</sub> f<sub>ac</sub>t<sub>s an</sub>d th<sub>e neu</sub>t<sub>ra</sub>l l<sub>a</sub>b<sub>e</sub>l <sub>space, no</sub> <sub>warran</sub>t<sub>,</sub> <sub>no</sub> <sub>pre</sub>f<sub>erre</sub>d <sub>a</sub>lt<sub>erna</sub>ti<sub>ve,</sub> i<sub>n</sub>d<sub>epen</sub>d<sub>en</sub>tl<sub>y</sub> <sub>ass</sub>i<sub>gns</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>pa</sub>th<sub>way</sub> <sub>an</sub>d b<sub>o</sub>ttl<sub>enec</sub>k<sub>,</sub> <sub>agree</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> <sub>w</sub>ith th<sub>e</sub> <sub>co</sub>d<sub>e</sub>d l<sub>a</sub>b<sub>e</sub>l<sub>s</sub> <sub>on</sub> endpoint→pathway 0.81 and pathway→bottleneck 0.77 (all five edges 36/57, 0.63 [0.50, 0.75]; Table A24). This is intermodel agreement given the coded chain, not raw-fact recov-<sub>ery,</sub> th<sub>e</sub> h<sub>ar</sub>d<sub>er neu</sub>t<sub>ra</sub>l<sub>-</sub>f<sub>ac</sub>t<sub>s</sub> t<sub>es</sub>t i<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e go</sub>ld <sub>anc</sub>h<sub>or</sub> b<sub>e</sub>l<sub>ow.</sub> W<sub>e</sub> d<sub>o</sub> <sub>no</sub>t f<sub>orce</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>res</sub>t t<sub>o</sub> <sub>pass:</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>rema</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> 30 <sub>c</sub>h<sub>a</sub>i<sub>ns</sub> <sub>are</sub> released as exploratory (Table A19).

Internal sanity checks (the counterfactual sweep recov-<sub>er</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> th<sub>e co</sub>d<sub>e</sub>d b<sub>o</sub>ttl<sub>enec</sub>k<sub>, repa</sub>i<sub>r</sub> di<sub>scr</sub>i<sub>m</sub>i<sub>na</sub>ti<sub>on,</sub> th<sub>e new-</sub> p<sup>ath</sup>w<sup>a</sup>y <sup>tri</sup>gg<sup>er</sup>, g<sup>ro</sup>u<sup>ndin</sup>g, <sup>and clos</sup>u<sup>re-a</sup>gg<sup>re</sup>g<sup>ator in</sup>v<sup>ari-</sup> ance) all <sub>p</sub>ass as ex<sub>p</sub>ected unit tests (Table A20, a<sub>pp</sub>endix).

The test is whether coupling the stages, driving <sub>a</sub>d<sub>op</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> f<sub>rom</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>evo</sub>l<sub>v</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> <sub>s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e,</sub> <sub>no</sub>t <sub>a</sub> <sub>per-case</sub> <sub>cons</sub>t<sub>an</sub>t<sub>,</sub> <sub>p</sub>reserves accurac<sub>y</sub>. Forward 2026- 2030 were conditional stress-tests: under a cyber incident, l<sub>a</sub>b<sub>or</sub> di<sub>sp</sub>l<sub>acemen</sub>t<sub>, or an</sub> i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>erna</sub>ti<sub>ona</sub>l <sub>race, consumer mea-</sub> <sub>sures</sub> <sub>s</sub>t<sub>ay</sub> f<sub>eas</sub>ibl<sub>e</sub> <sub>w</sub>hil<sub>e</sub> f<sub>ron</sub>ti<sub>er-sa</sub>f<sub>e</sub>t<sub>y</sub> <sub>con</sub>t<sub>ro</sub>l<sub>s</sub> <sub>s</sub>t<sub>ay</sub> h<sub>ar</sub>d<sub>.</sub> Policy takeaway: GPS reproduces recent governance history <sub>c</sub>l<sub>ose</sub>l<sub>y enoug</sub>h f<sub>or scenar</sub>i<sub>o ana</sub>l<sub>ys</sub>i<sub>s, s</sub>t<sub>ress-</sub>t<sub>es</sub>ti<sub>ng w</sub>hi<sub>c</sub>h <sub>ru</sub>l<sub>es s</sub>t<sub>ay</sub> f<sub>eas</sub>ibl<sub>e un</sub>d<sub>er a s</sub>h<sub>oc</sub>k<sub>, no</sub>t <sub>pre</sub>di<sub>c</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub>

Does GPS Recommend Sensible Policies? GPS ultimately claims to help choose measures, so we test that endto-end. On 15 decision sets (a <sub>g</sub>oal and three candidate mea-<sub>sures eac</sub>h<sub>, across compu</sub>t<sub>e, e</sub>l<sub>ec</sub>ti<sub>ons,</sub> l<sub>a</sub>b<sub>or, mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l <sub>re</sub>l<sub>ease,</sub> hirin<sub>g</sub>, medical AI, and more), GPS ranks candidates b<sub>y</sub> its objective while five single-lens experts (economist, legal, securit<sub>y</sub>, safet<sub>y</sub>, <sub>p</sub>olitical) each <sub>p</sub>ick from their remit alone. Whether it tracks human judgment needs <sub>a</sub> h<sub>uman s</sub>t<sub>u</sub>d<sub>y we</sub> h<sub>ave no</sub>t <sub>run,</sub> th<sub>e resu</sub>lt’<sub>s cen</sub>t<sub>ra</sub>l li<sub>m</sub>i<sub>-</sub> tation. Addin<sub>g</sub> uniform noise to ever<sub>y</sub> coded in<sub>p</sub>ut (salience, im<sub>p</sub>lementation, risk-reduction) and re-rankin<sub>g</sub> over 400 trials per set, GPS’s top-1 choice is unchanged in 99.9% of trials at ±0.05, 98.5% at ±0.10, 94.4% at ±0.15, and 89.1% at ±0.20. Swapping the multiplicative form for a min-link form reproduces all 15/15 winners and an additive form $1 2 / 1 5 ,$ <sub>, so</sub> th<sub>e ran</sub>ki<sub>ng</sub> i<sub>s</sub> l<sub>arge</sub>l<sub>y</sub> f<sub>orm-</sub>i<sub>n</sub>d<sub>epen</sub>d<sub>en</sub>t<sub>.</sub> O<sub>n</sub>l<sub>y</sub> t<sub>wo</sub> sets are genuinely fragile (top-1 flips in ≥10% of ±0.10 trials): election-dee<sub>p</sub>fake <sub>g</sub>overnance and AI incident res<sub>p</sub>onse, <sub>w</sub>h<sub>ere</sub> t<sub>wo</sub> <sub>can</sub>did<sub>a</sub>t<sub>es</sub> <sub>score</sub> <sub>near</sub>l<sub>y</sub> <sub>equa</sub>ll<sub>y,</sub> <sub>we</sub> fl<sub>ag</sub> th<sub>ese</sub> <sub>ra</sub>th<sub>er</sub> th<sub>an presen</sub>t <sub>a spur</sub>i<sub>ous w</sub>i<sub>nner.</sub> Thi<sub>s</sub> d<sub>oes no</sub>t <sub>ma</sub>k<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e</sub> coded inputs objective; it shows the ranking does not hinge on their exact values, and the objective’s weights should be <sub>se</sub>t b<sub>y</sub> th<sub>e</sub> d<sub>ep</sub>l<sub>oy</sub>i<sub>ng o</sub>fi<sub>ce, no</sub>t <sub>us.</sub>

Adoption prompt-sensitivity. On the 57 corpus cases, a blind model <sub>g</sub>i<sub>ven</sub> <sub>on</sub>l<sub>y</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>neu</sub>t<sub>ra</sub>l <sub>summary</sub> id<sub>en</sub>tifi<sub>es</sub> th<sub>e</sub> i<sub>nvo</sub>l<sub>ve</sub>d <sub>ro</sub>l<sub>e-</sub> level actor subsets (A1-A10) at recall 0.94, precision 0.59, F1 0.73 (117 hits, 8 misses, 80 over-inclusions): it almost never <sub>m</sub>i<sub>sses an</sub> i<sub>nvo</sub>l<sub>ve</sub>d <sub>ac</sub>t<sub>or</sub> b<sub>u</sub>t t<sub>en</sub>d<sub>s</sub> t<sub>o over-</sub>i<sub>nc</sub>l<sub>u</sub>d<sub>e p</sub>l<sub>aus</sub>ibl<sub>e</sub> ones, the safe error direction for a tool whose job is to not <sub>over</sub>l<sub>oo</sub>k <sub>an</sub> <sub>a</sub>f<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>par</sub>t<sub>y.</sub>

## O Benchmark Protocol and Leakage Control

Compute reporting. Policy: “large AI training runs must be reported by domestic cloud providers.” Chain: reporting dut<sub>y</sub> binds onl<sub>y</sub> domestic hosts → a develo<sub>p</sub>er rents an ofshore <sub>p</sub>rovider outside the dut<sub>y</sub> → the domestic re<sub>p</sub>ortin<sub>g</sub> re<sub>q</sub>uirement cannot observe com<sub>p</sub>ute it does not host → a frontier run proceeds unseen → foreign free-riding → coverage. Reasoning: the measure defines its reach by hosting l<sub>oca</sub>ti<sub>on,</sub> <sub>no</sub>t b<sub>y</sub> <sub>w</sub>h<sub>o</sub> t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>ns,</sub> <sub>so</sub> it<sub>s</sub> <sub>scope</sub> i<sub>s</sub> <sub>se</sub>t <sub>a</sub>t <sub>s</sub>t<sub>ep</sub> <sub>one.</sub> C<sub>ompu</sub>t<sub>e</sub> i<sub>s por</sub>t<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e, so a</sub> d<sub>eve</sub>l<sub>oper</sub> f<sub>ac</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> th<sub>e</sub> d<sub>u</sub>t<sub>y can s</sub>hift th<sub>e run a</sub>b<sub>roa</sub>d<sub>;</sub> th<sub>e capa</sub>bilit<sub>y moves ou</sub>t<sub>s</sub>id<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e covere</sub>d <sub>se</sub>t<sub>.</sub> A <sub>repor</sub>ti<sub>ng</sub> d<sub>u</sub>t<sub>y</sub> i<sub>s an o</sub>b<sub>serva</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> h<sub>oo</sub>k <sub>a</sub>tt<sub>ac</sub>h<sub>e</sub>d t<sub>o</sub> th<sub>e</sub> d<sub>omes</sub>ti<sub>c</sub> h<sub>os</sub>t<sub>, w</sub>h<sub>en</sub> th<sub>e</sub> h<sub>os</sub>t i<sub>s o</sub>f<sub>s</sub>h<sub>ore</sub> th<sub>ere</sub> i<sub>s no</sub>thi<sub>ng</sub> to trigger it, so the control fails by absence, not weakness. At end<sub>p</sub>oint→<sub>p</sub>athwa<sub>y</sub> the decisive feature is that the actor is outside jurisdiction (forei<sub>g</sub>n free-ridin<sub>g</sub>), not that a visible violation went un<sub>p</sub>unished (enforcement <sub>g</sub>a<sub>p</sub>): enforcem<sub>e</sub>nt n<sub>eve</sub>r <sub>e</sub>n<sub>gages</sub> b<sub>ecause</sub> n<sub>o</sub>thin<sub>g</sub> i<sub>s see</sub>n<sub>.</sub> H<sub>e</sub>n<sub>ce cove</sub>r<sub>age,</sub> not enforcement, is the bottleneck. Repair: targeted, recipro-<sub>ca</sub>l i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>erna</sub>ti<sub>ona</sub>l <sub>repor</sub>ti<sub>ng or c</sub>l<sub>ou</sub>d<sub>-prov</sub>id<sub>er</sub> KYC th<sub>a</sub>t f<sub>o</sub>l<sub>-</sub> l<sub>ows</sub> th<sub>e</sub> d<sub>eve</sub>l<sub>oper o</sub>f<sub>s</sub>h<sub>ore; wea</sub>k<sub>,</sub> hi<sub>g</sub>h<sub>er</sub> d<sub>omes</sub>ti<sub>c pena</sub>lti<sub>es,</sub> <sub>w</sub>hi<sub>c</sub>h <sub>ra</sub>i<sub>se cos</sub>t <sub>on</sub>l<sub>y</sub> f<sub>or</sub> th<sub>e a</sub>l<sub>rea</sub>d<sub>y-covere</sub>d <sub>se</sub>t<sub>.</sub>

Incident reporting. Policy: “frontier developers must report serious incidents to a public authority.” Chain: the duty <sub>y</sub>ields information but no res<sub>p</sub>onse <sub>p</sub>ower → a develo<sub>p</sub>er kee<sub>p</sub>s a harmful s<sub>y</sub>stem de<sub>p</sub>lo<sub>y</sub>ed after re<sub>p</sub>ortin<sub>g</sub> → re<sub>p</sub>orti<sub>ng</sub> i<sub>s a v</sub>i<sub>s</sub>ibilit<sub>y con</sub>t<sub>ro</sub>l<sub>, no</sub>t <sub>an</sub> i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>erven</sub>ti<sub>on con</sub>t<sub>ro</sub>l<sub>, so</sub> it cannot com<sub>p</sub>el a <sub>p</sub>ause → harm continues after it is observed → enforcement gap → enforcement.
